    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… they brought me in because of what happened at the fair. I was giving a talk there, you know, around the equipment, and I got up onto the tractor in the middle of it. I was trying to show everybody this new pattern I was seeing—how it all fit together—and I started jerking the controls around to demonstrate it.\n\nPeople were yelling at me to stop, and I heard them, I did, but I just… I couldn’t make myself stop right then. It felt like I had to keep showing it, like if I stopped they wouldn’t understand. So that’s why I’m here, I guess. They got pretty worried after that.\n\nDoctor: Did you really see a pattern that you were trying to show everybody when you were on the tractor?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I mean, maybe “see” isn’t the right word like seeing a cow in a field, but yes, there was a pattern there. It’s in the numbers. The odd numbers keep showing up in the yield maps, and then the same sort of thing turns up in those drone reports—places where the rows don’t line up right, or the figures come back looking too specific to be random.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me, “Look, it’s just the lender’s paperwork, it’s plain as day,” and she lays it out on the kitchen table like that settles it. But I know what plain paperwork looks like. I’ve dealt with lenders and crop reports and maps my whole life. This is different. Those odd numbers are coded warnings. They’re telling me people are trying to take the farm out from under me, and nobody wants to admit it because then they’d have to say who’s behind it.\n\nSo on the tractor, I was trying to show them how the pieces fit together. I know it looked bad, me moving the controls like that, but I wasn’t just fooling around. I was trying to make them understand before it was too late.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I still believe it. I know how that sounds when I say it straight out, and my wife gets that look like I’m being stubborn or chasing shadows, but I’ve gone over those papers and those maps more times than I can count.\n\nShe’ll sit there and show me the lender’s paperwork, and she says, “This is all it is, it’s right here in black and white.” And I’m not saying the paperwork isn’t real. I know what loan papers look like. I’ve signed enough of them. But that’s not the whole of it. The odd numbers in the yield maps, and then the same kind of numbers turning up again in the drone reports—those aren’t just mistakes or coincidence. They’re too lined up. They’re warnings, coded warnings, like somebody is leaving tracks but trying not to be seen doing it.\n\nAnd it all points the same way to me: folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. Maybe they think I won’t notice because they can bury it in reports and figures, but I do notice. I can’t just ignore it because everyone says it’s plain paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see these coded warnings in the numbers that you're talking about?\n\nPatient: I think they could see it if they’d look at it the right way. That’s the part that frustrates me. It’s not like I’m saying the numbers are floating in the air or something only I can physically see. They’re right there in the yield maps, and they’re right there again in the drone reports. Other people can look at the same pages.\n\nBut when my wife looks, she just points back to the lender’s paperwork and says it’s ordinary, plain business, nothing hidden. And I’m telling you, I know the paperwork is there, I know what she’s showing me. I’m not confused about that part. I just don’t think she’s seeing what’s underneath it. Those odd numbers keep lining up too neatly, and to me that means they’re coded warnings—warnings that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo… can other people see them? They can see the numbers, yes. But they don’t seem to understand what they mean. Or maybe they don’t want to say they do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ve had times where I tried to set it down, if that’s what you mean. Like after my wife gets all the lender’s papers out and shows me the figures and the signatures and says, “See, this is just normal paperwork,” I’ll sit there and think, alright, maybe I should just let it be for tonight.\n\nBut then I look back at the yield maps, and the odd numbers are still there. And then I think about the drone reports, how the same kind of numbers show up again, and it doesn’t feel like something I can just talk myself out of. It’s not like I want to believe people are trying to take the farm out from under me. That’s a terrible thing to carry around. But when it lines up like that, over and over, I know it means something.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was a stretch where I honestly thought, “No, those aren’t warnings, that’s just random,” then… no. I don’t think so. I may have kept quiet about it, or tried not to argue with my wife about it, but I didn’t stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your interpretation of the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: I’ve had moments where I wished I had doubts, if that makes sense. Like when my wife lays everything out on the table, the lender’s paperwork, the regular figures, the plain explanations, I can sit there and think, alright, maybe this is what they want me to see as ordinary business. And I don’t want to argue with her every time, because she gets tired, and I get tired too.\n\nBut real doubts? No, not in the way you mean. I keep coming back to the same thing: those odd numbers in the yield maps, and then the drone reports showing the same kind of thing. It’s too specific. It feels like a pattern that’s been put there, like coded warnings. And to me, those warnings are about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might hesitate saying it out loud because I know how it sounds. I might try to step away from it for a bit. But when I look at the numbers again, I know what they are. My wife sees paperwork. I see what’s underneath it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: About… I’d say about 11 months now. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first it wasn’t like I sat up one morning and said, “There, that’s what this is.” It sort of built up. I started noticing the odd numbers in the yield maps, and then when I saw similar things in the drone reports, it got harder and harder to ignore.\n\nBut if you’re asking how long I’ve believed they were coded warnings about someone trying to take the farm, then yes, about 11 months. That’s when it really settled in for me that it wasn’t just ordinary figures on a page.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been even a day where I truly didn’t think that. I’ve had days where I tried not to look at the maps, or I told myself, just get through chores, don’t start in on it again, don’t upset everybody. And my wife, she’ll sit me down with the lender’s papers and point to the plain parts — signatures, totals, dates, all that — and she’ll say, “See, this is just what they send everybody.”\n\nAnd I hear her. I do. I’m not saying I don’t hear her. But it doesn’t change what I see in the yield maps, and then in the drone reports too. Those odd numbers show up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental. They’re warnings. They’re coded warnings about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might have a quiet day, or a day where I don’t say much about it. But a day where I actually believed, “No, that’s not what this is”? No. I can’t say I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment when your belief in the coded warnings completely changed, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… not completely. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get worn down and I think, “Maybe I should just let it alone for tonight,” especially when my wife has the lender’s paperwork spread out in front of me and she’s showing me the regular parts of it, the plain numbers and signatures and dates and all that. She’ll say, “Look, this is just paperwork, this is just what they send,” and I can sit there and nod because I don’t want another fight.\n\nBut inside, it doesn’t really change. Not all the way. Even when she shows me that plain paperwork, I still know what I saw in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers aren’t just nothing to me. They line up too much. They feel like coded warnings, like somebody’s trying to tell me—or maybe warn me—that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo if you mean have I gone soft on it for a little while, or kept quiet, yes, I suppose. But if you mean did I ever actually stop believing it, even briefly… no. It’s still there. It doesn’t switch off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the coded warnings is part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do think it’s part of something bigger. I don’t know all the pieces, and that’s what bothers me so much, because I can see enough of it to know it isn’t random, but not enough to know exactly who is moving what around.\n\nWhen my wife shows me the lender’s paperwork, the regular paperwork, I understand what she’s trying to do. She’ll point out the plain numbers, the dates, the signatures, the normal language, and she’ll say, “See, this is just business, this is just forms.” And I want to believe that would settle it. I really do, because it would be easier on everybody if I could just look at it and say, fine, nothing’s happening.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, and then the drone reports, and those odd numbers keep showing up in ways that don’t feel ordinary. It feels like a code. Like warnings tucked into the farm data where someone like me would see it if I was paying attention. And to me, the warning is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, yes, I think there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I haven’t figured out the whole thing, but I feel like I’ve figured out enough to know the maps and reports are telling me something.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the odd numbers are coded warnings ever completely gone away, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t gone away. I know I probably sound like I’m repeating myself, but that’s the truth of it. But completely gone away? No. I don’t think it has.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you didn't believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been even a brief moment where I didn’t believe it at all. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. Or maybe they don’t want to say they do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ve had times where I tried to set it down, if that’s what you mean. Like after my wife gets all the lender’s papers out and shows me the figures and the signatures and says, “See, this is just normal paperwork,” I’ll sit there and think, alright, maybe I should just let it be for tonight.\n\nBut then I look back at the yield maps, and the odd numbers are still there. And then I think about the drone reports, how the same kind of numbers show up again, and it doesn’t feel like something I can just talk myself out of. It’s not like I want to believe people are trying to take the farm out from under me. That’s a terrible thing to carry around. But when it lines up like that, over and over, I know it means something.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was a stretch where I honestly thought, “No, those aren’t warnings, that’s just random,” then… no. I don’t think so. I may have kept quiet about it, or tried not to argue with my wife about it, but I didn’t stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your interpretation of the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: I’ve had moments where I wished I had doubts, if that makes sense. Like when my wife lays everything out on the table, the lender’s paperwork, the regular figures, the plain explanations, I can sit there and think, alright, maybe this is what they want me to see as ordinary business. And I don’t want to argue with her every time, because she gets tired, and I get tired too.\n\nBut real doubts? No, not in the way you mean. I keep coming back to the same thing: those odd numbers in the yield maps, and then the drone reports showing the same kind of thing. It’s too specific. It feels like a pattern that’s been put there, like coded warnings. And to me, those warnings are about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might hesitate saying it out loud because I know how it sounds. I might try to step away from it for a bit. But when I look at the numbers again, I know what they are. My wife sees paperwork. I see what’s underneath it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: About… I’d say about 11 months now. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first it wasn’t like I sat up one morning and said, “There, that’s what this is.” It sort of built up. I started noticing the odd numbers in the yield maps, and then when I saw similar things in the drone reports, it got harder and harder to ignore.\n\nBut if you’re asking how long I’ve believed they were coded warnings about someone trying to take the farm, then yes, about 11 months. That’s when it really settled in for me that it wasn’t just ordinary figures on a page.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been even a day where I truly didn’t think that. I’ve had days where I tried not to look at the maps, or I told myself, just get through chores, don’t start in on it again, don’t upset everybody. And my wife, she’ll sit me down with the lender’s papers and point to the plain parts — signatures, totals, dates, all that — and she’ll say, “See, this is just what they send everybody.”\n\nAnd I hear her. I do. I’m not saying I don’t hear her. But it doesn’t change what I see in the yield maps, and then in the drone reports too. Those odd numbers show up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental. They’re warnings. They’re coded warnings about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might have a quiet day, or a day where I don’t say much about it. But a day where I actually believed, “No, that’s not what this is”? No. I can’t say I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment when your belief in the coded warnings completely changed, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… not completely. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get worn down and I think, “Maybe I should just let it alone for tonight,” especially when my wife has the lender’s paperwork spread out in front of me and she’s showing me the regular parts of it, the plain numbers and signatures and dates and all that. She’ll say, “Look, this is just paperwork, this is just what they send,” and I can sit there and nod because I don’t want another fight.\n\nBut inside, it doesn’t really change. Not all the way. Even when she shows me that plain paperwork, I still know what I saw in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers aren’t just nothing to me. They line up too much. They feel like coded warnings, like somebody’s trying to tell me—or maybe warn me—that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo if you mean have I gone soft on it for a little while, or kept quiet, yes, I suppose. But if you mean did I ever actually stop believing it, even briefly… no. It’s still there. It doesn’t switch off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the coded warnings is part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do think it’s part of something bigger. I don’t know all the pieces, and that’s what bothers me so much, because I can see enough of it to know it isn’t random, but not enough to know exactly who is moving what around.\n\nWhen my wife shows me the lender’s paperwork, the regular paperwork, I understand what she’s trying to do. She’ll point out the plain numbers, the dates, the signatures, the normal language, and she’ll say, “See, this is just business, this is just forms.” And I want to believe that would settle it. I really do, because it would be easier on everybody if I could just look at it and say, fine, nothing’s happening.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, and then the drone reports, and those odd numbers keep showing up in ways that don’t feel ordinary. It feels like a code. Like warnings tucked into the farm data where someone like me would see it if I was paying attention. And to me, the warning is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, yes, I think there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I haven’t figured out the whole thing, but I feel like I’ve figured out enough to know the maps and reports are telling me something.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the odd numbers are coded warnings ever completely gone away, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t gone away. I know I probably sound like I’m repeating myself, but that’s the truth of it. Even when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender’s paperwork — the plain paperwork, the kind with the dates and signatures and regular numbers — I can look at it and understand what she’s pointing to. I can see that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers don’t feel random to me. They show up like warnings, like coded warnings. And to me, they’re about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nI might stop talking about it for a while, or I might try to leave it alone so we can get through supper or get some sleep. But completely gone away? No. I don’t think it has.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you didn't believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been even a brief moment where I didn’t believe it at all. I know we keep circling that question, but I’m trying to answer it straight.\n\nThere are times I get tired of arguing about it, or I sit there while my wife lays out the lender’s paperwork and points to the plain parts — the dates, the signatures, the regular numbers — and I can see what she means on the surface. I can understand why she says, “This is just paperwork.” I’m not saying I can’t read what’s in front of me.\n\nBut it doesn’t undo what I know from the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. And the warning is about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had moments where I shut my mouth about it, or tried to act like I was letting it go so the house could be quiet for a while. But not believing it at all? No. I can’t honestly say that’s ever happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people trying to take your farm are connected to any other events or conspiracies in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it other conspiracies exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than I can prove. But it does feel connected to the farm and the people around the farm business, yes.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is this: my wife can show me the lender’s paperwork, the plain paperwork, and I can sit there and look at the numbers they put on the page, the signatures, the terms, all of that. I understand what she’s saying when she says it’s just normal lender business. I’m not blind to what’s on the paper.\n\nBut then those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports line up in a way that feels like they’re meant to be read. Like somebody put warnings there because they knew I’d see them if I looked close enough. And the warning is always pointing the same direction to me — that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, connected to everything in my life? I don’t know. I don’t want to say that. But connected to the farm, the reports, the lender stuff, the people who would benefit if I lost it? Yes. That part feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in the coded warnings ever wavered to the point where you questioned its validity for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, not for more than a few hours. I mean… I can sit there and question myself in the sense that I know how it sounds to other people. My wife will put the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’ll say, “Look, this is plain as day, this is just the loan terms, this is just the schedule, these are normal figures.” And I can follow that. I can see the regular paperwork. They don’t read like accidents to me. They’re not just numbers that happened to fall that way. They feel placed. They feel like warnings, like somebody knew I’d understand what was being shown.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had a spell where I’m tired, or I try to tell myself, “Leave it alone for now, don’t stir things up,” especially if my wife is upset or we’re just trying to get through the day. No. I know that’s probably frustrating because you’re asking it very plainly, but no, I can’t think of one single moment where I didn’t believe it at all.

I can have moments where I’m worn down, or where my wife is sitting at the kitchen table with the lender’s papers spread out, pointing at the regular figures and saying, “See, this is just ordinary paperwork.” And I can understand the plain part of that. I can see the dates, the terms, the signatures, all the normal-looking numbers they want a person to focus on.

But that doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers are still there, and they don’t feel random to me. They feel coded. They feel like warnings meant to tell me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.

So I might get quiet about it. I might stop arguing for a while because I’m tired or because I don’t want another fight. But not believe it, even for one moment? No. I can’t honestly say that.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… they brought me in because of what happened at the fair. I was giving a talk there, you know, around the equipment, and I got up onto the tractor in the middle of it. It felt like I had to keep showing it, like if I stopped they wouldn’t understand. So that’s why I’m here, I guess. They got pretty worried after that.\n\nDoctor: Did you really see a pattern that you were trying to show everybody when you were on the tractor?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I mean, maybe “see” isn’t the right word like seeing a cow in a field, but yes, there was a pattern there. It’s in the numbers. The odd numbers keep showing up in the yield maps, and then the same sort of thing turns up in those drone reports—places where the rows don’t line up right, or the figures come back looking too specific to be random.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me, “Look, it’s just the lender’s paperwork, it’s plain as day,” and she lays it out on the kitchen table like that settles it. I know what loan papers look like. I’ve signed enough of them. But that’s not the whole of it. They can see the numbers, yes. But they don’t seem to understand what they mean. I mean, I’ve had times where I tried to set it down, if that’s what you mean. And then I think about the drone reports, how the same kind of numbers show up again, and it doesn’t feel like something I can just talk myself out of. It’s not like I want to believe people are trying to take the farm out from under me. That’s a terrible thing to carry around. I don’t think so. I may have kept quiet about it, or tried not to argue with my wife about it, but I didn’t stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your interpretation of the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: I’ve had moments where I wished I had doubts, if that makes sense. Like when my wife lays everything out on the table, the lender’s paperwork, the regular figures, the plain explanations, I can sit there and think, alright, maybe this is what they want me to see as ordinary business. And I don’t want to argue with her every time, because she gets tired, and I get tired too.\n\nBut real doubts? No, not in the way you mean. I keep coming back to the same thing: those odd numbers in the yield maps, and then the drone reports showing the same kind of thing. It’s too specific. It feels like a pattern that’s been put there, like coded warnings. And to me, those warnings are about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might hesitate saying it out loud because I know how it sounds. I might try to step away from it for a bit. But when I look at the numbers again, I know what they are. My wife sees paperwork. I see what’s underneath it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: About… I’d say about 11 months now. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first it wasn’t like I sat up one morning and said, “There, that’s what this is.” It sort of built up. I started noticing the odd numbers in the yield maps, and then when I saw similar things in the drone reports, it got harder and harder to ignore.\n\nBut if you’re asking how long I’ve believed they were coded warnings about someone trying to take the farm, then yes, about 11 months. That’s when it really settled in for me that it wasn’t just ordinary figures on a page.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been even a day where I truly didn’t think that. I’ve had days where I tried not to look at the maps, or I told myself, just get through chores, don’t start in on it again, don’t upset everybody. And my wife, she’ll sit me down with the lender’s papers and point to the plain parts — signatures, totals, dates, all that — and she’ll say, “See, this is just what they send everybody.”\n\nAnd I hear her. I do. I’m not saying I don’t hear her. But it doesn’t change what I see in the yield maps, and then in the drone reports too. Those odd numbers show up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental. They’re warnings. They’re coded warnings about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might have a quiet day, or a day where I don’t say much about it. But a day where I actually believed, “No, that’s not what this is”? No. I can’t say I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment when your belief in the coded warnings completely changed, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… not completely. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get worn down and I think, “Maybe I should just let it alone for tonight,” especially when my wife has the lender’s paperwork spread out in front of me and she’s showing me the regular parts of it, the plain numbers and signatures and dates and all that. She’ll say, “Look, this is just paperwork, this is just what they send,” and I can sit there and nod because I don’t want another fight.\n\nBut inside, it doesn’t really change. Not all the way. Even when she shows me that plain paperwork, I still know what I saw in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers aren’t just nothing to me. They line up too much. They feel like coded warnings, like somebody’s trying to tell me—or maybe warn me—that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo if you mean have I gone soft on it for a little while, or kept quiet, yes, I suppose. But if you mean did I ever actually stop believing it, even briefly… no. It’s still there. It doesn’t switch off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the coded warnings is part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do think it’s part of something bigger. I don’t know all the pieces, and that’s what bothers me so much, because I can see enough of it to know it isn’t random, but not enough to know exactly who is moving what around.\n\nWhen my wife shows me the lender’s paperwork, the regular paperwork, I understand what she’s trying to do. She’ll point out the plain numbers, the dates, the signatures, the normal language, and she’ll say, “See, this is just business, this is just forms.” And I want to believe that would settle it. I really do, because it would be easier on everybody if I could just look at it and say, fine, nothing’s happening.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, and then the drone reports, and those odd numbers keep showing up in ways that don’t feel ordinary. It feels like a code. Like warnings tucked into the farm data where someone like me would see it if I was paying attention. And to me, the warning is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, yes, I think there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I haven’t figured out the whole thing, but I feel like I’ve figured out enough to know the maps and reports are telling me something.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the odd numbers are coded warnings ever completely gone away, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t gone away. I know I probably sound like I’m repeating myself, but that’s the truth of it. Even when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender’s paperwork — the plain paperwork, the kind with the dates and signatures and regular numbers — I can look at it and understand what she’s pointing to. I can see that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers don’t feel random to me. They show up like warnings, like coded warnings. And to me, they’re about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nI might stop talking about it for a while, or I might try to leave it alone so we can get through supper or get some sleep. But completely gone away? No. I don’t think it has.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you didn't believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been even a brief moment where I didn’t believe it at all. I know we keep circling that question, but I’m trying to answer it straight.\n\nThere are times I get tired of arguing about it, or I sit there while my wife lays out the lender’s paperwork and points to the plain parts — the dates, the signatures, the regular numbers — and I can see what she means on the surface. I can understand why she says, “This is just paperwork.” I’m not saying I can’t read what’s in front of me.\n\nBut it doesn’t undo what I know from the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. And the warning is about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had moments where I shut my mouth about it, or tried to act like I was letting it go so the house could be quiet for a while. But not believing it at all? No. I can’t honestly say that’s ever happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people trying to take your farm are connected to any other events or conspiracies in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it other conspiracies exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than I can prove. But it does feel connected to the farm and the people around the farm business, yes.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is this: my wife can show me the lender’s paperwork, the plain paperwork, and I can sit there and look at the numbers they put on the page, the signatures, the terms, all of that. I understand what she’s saying when she says it’s just normal lender business. I’m not blind to what’s on the paper.\n\nBut then those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports line up in a way that feels like they’re meant to be read. Like somebody put warnings there because they knew I’d see them if I looked close enough. And the warning is always pointing the same direction to me — that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, connected to everything in my life? I don’t know. I don’t want to say that. But connected to the farm, the reports, the lender stuff, the people who would benefit if I lost it? Yes. That part feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in the coded warnings ever wavered to the point where you questioned its validity for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, not for more than a few hours. I mean… I can sit there and question myself in the sense that I know how it sounds to other people. My wife will put the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’ll say, “Look, this is plain as day, this is just the loan terms, this is just the schedule, these are normal figures.” And I can follow that. I can see the regular paperwork. I can see why she thinks it should settle the matter.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, or I look over the drone reports, and those odd numbers are still there. They don’t read like accidents to me. They’re not just numbers that happened to fall that way. They feel placed. They feel like warnings, like somebody knew I’d understand what was being shown.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had a spell where I’m tired, or I try to tell myself, “Leave it alone for now, don’t stir things up,” especially if my wife is upset or we’re just trying to get through the day. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. And the warning is about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had moments where I shut my mouth about it, or tried to act like I was letting it go so the house could be quiet for a while. But not believing it at all? No. I can’t honestly say that’s ever happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people trying to take your farm are connected to any other events or conspiracies in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it other conspiracies exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than I can prove. But it does feel connected to the farm and the people around the farm business, yes.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is this: my wife can show me the lender’s paperwork, the plain paperwork, and I can sit there and look at the numbers they put on the page, the signatures, the terms, all of that. I understand what she’s saying when she says it’s just normal lender business. I’m not blind to what’s on the paper.\n\nBut then those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports line up in a way that feels like they’re meant to be read. Like somebody put warnings there because they knew I’d see them if I looked close enough. And the warning is always pointing the same direction to me — that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, connected to everything in my life? I don’t know. I don’t want to say that. But connected to the farm, the reports, the lender stuff, the people who would benefit if I lost it? Yes. That part feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in the coded warnings ever wavered to the point where you questioned its validity for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, not for more than a few hours. I mean… I can sit there and question myself in the sense that I know how it sounds to other people. My wife will put the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’ll say, “Look, this is plain as day, this is just the loan terms, this is just the schedule, these are normal figures.” And I can follow that. I can see the regular paperwork. I can see why she thinks it should settle the matter.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, or I look over the drone reports, and those odd numbers are still there. They don’t read like accidents to me. They’re not just numbers that happened to fall that way. They feel placed. They feel like warnings, like somebody knew I’d understand what was being shown.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had a spell where I’m tired, or I try to tell myself, “Leave it alone for now, don’t stir things up,” especially if my wife is upset or we’re just trying to get through the day. But questioning whether it’s valid for more than a few hours? No. It comes right back to the same thing for me — those coded warnings are there, and they’re about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a single moment when you did not believe the odd numbers were coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No. I know that’s probably frustrating because you’re asking it very plainly, but no, I can’t think of one single moment where I didn’t believe it at all.\n\nI can have moments where I’m worn down, or where my wife is sitting at the kitchen table with the lender’s papers spread out, pointing at the regular figures and saying, “See, this is just ordinary paperwork.” And I can understand the plain part of that. I can see the dates, the terms, the signatures, all the normal-looking numbers they want a person to focus on.\n\nBut that doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not for a whole day. I don’t think there’s been even close to that, really.

I mean, I’ve tried to put it down for a day, if that’s what you mean. Like when my wife is tired of hearing it, or she brings out the lender’s paperwork and says, “Here, look, this is all plain business, this is what they sent, this is what you signed, this is just the loan.” And I can sit there and follow what she’s showing me. I’m not saying the regular paperwork isn’t there. I see it.

But completely stop believing the odd numbers are warnings? No. Because when I look back at the yield maps, and when I think about the drone reports, those numbers still stand out. They still don’t look like ordinary mistakes or random figures to me. They feel like they were put there for a reason, like somebody knew I’d catch it if I looked close enough.

So maybe I’ve gone quiet about it for a day. Maybe I’ve tried not to bring it up, just to keep peace in the house. But inside, no, I haven’t stopped believing it. To me, those coded warnings are still pointing to the same thing — folks trying to take the farm out from under me.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… they brought me in because of what happened at the fair. I was giving a talk there, you know, around the equipment, and I got up onto the tractor in the middle of it. I was trying to show everybody this new pattern I was seeing—how it all fit together—and I started jerking the controls around to demonstrate it.\n\nPeople were yelling at me to stop, and I heard them, I did, but I just… I couldn’t make myself stop right then. It felt like I had to keep showing it, like if I stopped they wouldn’t understand. So that’s why I’m here, I guess. They got pretty worried after that.\n\nDoctor: Did you really see a pattern that you were trying to show everybody when you were on the tractor?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I mean, maybe “see” isn’t the right word like seeing a cow in a field, but yes, there was a pattern there. It’s in the numbers. The odd numbers keep showing up in the yield maps, and then the same sort of thing turns up in those drone reports—places where the rows don’t line up right, or the figures come back looking too specific to be random.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me, “Look, it’s just the lender’s paperwork, it’s plain as day,” and she lays it out on the kitchen table like that settles it. But I know what plain paperwork looks like. I’ve dealt with lenders and crop reports and maps my whole life. This is different. Those odd numbers are coded warnings. They’re telling me people are trying to take the farm out from under me, and nobody wants to admit it because then they’d have to say who’s behind it.\n\nSo on the tractor, I was trying to show them how the pieces fit together. I know it looked bad, me moving the controls like that, but I wasn’t just fooling around. I was trying to make them understand before it was too late.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I still believe it. I know how that sounds when I say it straight out, and my wife gets that look like I’m being stubborn or chasing shadows, but I’ve gone over those papers and those maps more times than I can count.\n\nShe’ll sit there and show me the lender’s paperwork, and she says, “This is all it is, it’s right here in black and white.” And I’m not saying the paperwork isn’t real. I know what loan papers look like. I’ve signed enough of them. But that’s not the whole of it. The odd numbers in the yield maps, and then the same kind of numbers turning up again in the drone reports—those aren’t just mistakes or coincidence. They’re too lined up. They’re warnings, coded warnings, like somebody is leaving tracks but trying not to be seen doing it.\n\nAnd it all points the same way to me: folks are trying to take the farm out from under me. Maybe they think I won’t notice because they can bury it in reports and figures, but I do notice. I can’t just ignore it because everyone says it’s plain paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see these coded warnings in the numbers that you're talking about?\n\nPatient: I think they could see it if they’d look at it the right way. That’s the part that frustrates me. It’s not like I’m saying the numbers are floating in the air or something only I can physically see. They’re right there in the yield maps, and they’re right there again in the drone reports. Other people can look at the same pages.\n\nBut when my wife looks, she just points back to the lender’s paperwork and says it’s ordinary, plain business, nothing hidden. And I’m telling you, I know the paperwork is there, I know what she’s showing me. I’m not confused about that part. I just don’t think she’s seeing what’s underneath it. Those odd numbers keep lining up too neatly, and to me that means they’re coded warnings—warnings that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo… can other people see them? They can see the numbers, yes. But they don’t seem to understand what they mean. Or maybe they don’t want to say they do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ve had times where I tried to set it down, if that’s what you mean. Like after my wife gets all the lender’s papers out and shows me the figures and the signatures and says, “See, this is just normal paperwork,” I’ll sit there and think, alright, maybe I should just let it be for tonight.\n\nBut then I look back at the yield maps, and the odd numbers are still there. And then I think about the drone reports, how the same kind of numbers show up again, and it doesn’t feel like something I can just talk myself out of. It’s not like I want to believe people are trying to take the farm out from under me. That’s a terrible thing to carry around. But when it lines up like that, over and over, I know it means something.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was a stretch where I honestly thought, “No, those aren’t warnings, that’s just random,” then… no. I don’t think so. I may have kept quiet about it, or tried not to argue with my wife about it, but I didn’t stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your interpretation of the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports?\n\nPatient: I’ve had moments where I wished I had doubts, if that makes sense. Like when my wife lays everything out on the table, the lender’s paperwork, the regular figures, the plain explanations, I can sit there and think, alright, maybe this is what they want me to see as ordinary business. And I don’t want to argue with her every time, because she gets tired, and I get tired too.\n\nBut real doubts? No, not in the way you mean. I keep coming back to the same thing: those odd numbers in the yield maps, and then the drone reports showing the same kind of thing. It’s too specific. It feels like a pattern that’s been put there, like coded warnings. And to me, those warnings are about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might hesitate saying it out loud because I know how it sounds. I might try to step away from it for a bit. But when I look at the numbers again, I know what they are. My wife sees paperwork. I see what’s underneath it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: About… I’d say about 11 months now. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first it wasn’t like I sat up one morning and said, “There, that’s what this is.” It sort of built up. I started noticing the odd numbers in the yield maps, and then when I saw similar things in the drone reports, it got harder and harder to ignore.\n\nBut if you’re asking how long I’ve believed they were coded warnings about someone trying to take the farm, then yes, about 11 months. That’s when it really settled in for me that it wasn’t just ordinary figures on a page.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been even a day where I truly didn’t think that. I’ve had days where I tried not to look at the maps, or I told myself, just get through chores, don’t start in on it again, don’t upset everybody. And my wife, she’ll sit me down with the lender’s papers and point to the plain parts — signatures, totals, dates, all that — and she’ll say, “See, this is just what they send everybody.”\n\nAnd I hear her. I do. I’m not saying I don’t hear her. But it doesn’t change what I see in the yield maps, and then in the drone reports too. Those odd numbers show up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental. They’re warnings. They’re coded warnings about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might have a quiet day, or a day where I don’t say much about it. But a day where I actually believed, “No, that’s not what this is”? No. I can’t say I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment when your belief in the coded warnings completely changed, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… not completely. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get worn down and I think, “Maybe I should just let it alone for tonight,” especially when my wife has the lender’s paperwork spread out in front of me and she’s showing me the regular parts of it, the plain numbers and signatures and dates and all that. She’ll say, “Look, this is just paperwork, this is just what they send,” and I can sit there and nod because I don’t want another fight.\n\nBut inside, it doesn’t really change. Not all the way. Even when she shows me that plain paperwork, I still know what I saw in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers aren’t just nothing to me. They line up too much. They feel like coded warnings, like somebody’s trying to tell me—or maybe warn me—that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo if you mean have I gone soft on it for a little while, or kept quiet, yes, I suppose. But if you mean did I ever actually stop believing it, even briefly… no. It’s still there. It doesn’t switch off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the coded warnings is part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do think it’s part of something bigger. I don’t know all the pieces, and that’s what bothers me so much, because I can see enough of it to know it isn’t random, but not enough to know exactly who is moving what around.\n\nWhen my wife shows me the lender’s paperwork, the regular paperwork, I understand what she’s trying to do. She’ll point out the plain numbers, the dates, the signatures, the normal language, and she’ll say, “See, this is just business, this is just forms.” And I want to believe that would settle it. I really do, because it would be easier on everybody if I could just look at it and say, fine, nothing’s happening.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, and then the drone reports, and those odd numbers keep showing up in ways that don’t feel ordinary. It feels like a code. Like warnings tucked into the farm data where someone like me would see it if I was paying attention. And to me, the warning is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, yes, I think there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I haven’t figured out the whole thing, but I feel like I’ve figured out enough to know the maps and reports are telling me something.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the odd numbers are coded warnings ever completely gone away, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t gone away. I know I probably sound like I’m repeating myself, but that’s the truth of it. Even when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender’s paperwork — the plain paperwork, the kind with the dates and signatures and regular numbers — I can look at it and understand what she’s pointing to. I can see that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers don’t feel random to me. They show up like warnings, like coded warnings. And to me, they’re about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nI might stop talking about it for a while, or I might try to leave it alone so we can get through supper or get some sleep. But completely gone away? No. I don’t think it has.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you didn't believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been even a brief moment where I didn’t believe it at all. I know we keep circling that question, but I’m trying to answer it straight.\n\nThere are times I get tired of arguing about it, or I sit there while my wife lays out the lender’s paperwork and points to the plain parts — the dates, the signatures, the regular numbers — and I can see what she means on the surface. I can understand why she says, “This is just paperwork.” I’m not saying I can’t read what’s in front of me.\n\nBut it doesn’t undo what I know from the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. And the warning is about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had moments where I shut my mouth about it, or tried to act like I was letting it go so the house could be quiet for a while. But not believing it at all? No. I can’t honestly say that’s ever happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people trying to take your farm are connected to any other events or conspiracies in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it other conspiracies exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than I can prove. But it does feel connected to the farm and the people around the farm business, yes.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is this: my wife can show me the lender’s paperwork, the plain paperwork, and I can sit there and look at the numbers they put on the page, the signatures, the terms, all of that. I understand what she’s saying when she says it’s just normal lender business. I’m not blind to what’s on the paper.\n\nBut then those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports line up in a way that feels like they’re meant to be read. Like somebody put warnings there because they knew I’d see them if I looked close enough. And the warning is always pointing the same direction to me — that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, connected to everything in my life? I don’t know. I don’t want to say that. But connected to the farm, the reports, the lender stuff, the people who would benefit if I lost it? Yes. That part feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in the coded warnings ever wavered to the point where you questioned its validity for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, not for more than a few hours. I mean… I can sit there and question myself in the sense that I know how it sounds to other people. My wife will put the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’ll say, “Look, this is plain as day, this is just the loan terms, this is just the schedule, these are normal figures.” And I can follow that. I can see the regular paperwork. I can see why she thinks it should settle the matter.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, or I look over the drone reports, and those odd numbers are still there. No… no, I don’t think there’s been any amount of time where I didn’t believe it at all. I know that sounds like I’m just being stubborn, but it’s not like that to me. It’s more like once I saw the pattern, I couldn’t unsee it.

I’m not saying I can’t read the plain paperwork. I know what a regular loan document looks like.

But the odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports… those are different. They stand out. They’re not just stray figures to me. They feel coded, like warnings. Like somebody put them there because they knew I’d know what they meant. And what they mean is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.

So I might stop talking about it for a little while, especially if my wife is upset or I can tell people are getting tired of hearing me explain it. I might try to sit still and keep my mouth shut. But inside, no — there hasn’t been even a short bit where I truly didn’t believe it.

Like when my wife lays everything out on the table, the lender’s paperwork, the regular figures, the plain explanations, I can sit there and think, alright, maybe this is what they want me to see as ordinary business. It feels like a pattern that’s been put there, like coded warnings. And to me, those warnings are about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might hesitate saying it out loud because I know how it sounds. I might try to step away from it for a bit. But when I look at the numbers again, I know what they are. My wife sees paperwork. I see what’s underneath it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: About… I’d say about 11 months now. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first it wasn’t like I sat up one morning and said, “There, that’s what this is.” It sort of built up. I started noticing the odd numbers in the yield maps, and then when I saw similar things in the drone reports, it got harder and harder to ignore.\n\nBut if you’re asking how long I’ve believed they were coded warnings about someone trying to take the farm, then yes, about 11 months. That’s when it really settled in for me that it wasn’t just ordinary figures on a page.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been even a day where I truly didn’t think that. I’ve had days where I tried not to look at the maps, or I told myself, just get through chores, don’t start in on it again, don’t upset everybody. But a day where I actually believed, “No, that’s not what this is”? No. I can’t say I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment when your belief in the coded warnings completely changed, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… not completely. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get worn down and I think, “Maybe I should just let it alone for tonight,” especially when my wife has the lender’s paperwork spread out in front of me and she’s showing me the regular parts of it, the plain numbers and signatures and dates and all that. She’ll say, “Look, this is just paperwork, this is just what they send,” and I can sit there and nod because I don’t want another fight.\n\nBut inside, it doesn’t really change. Not all the way. I don’t know all the pieces, and that’s what bothers me so much, because I can see enough of it to know it isn’t random, but not enough to know exactly who is moving what around.\n\nWhen my wife shows me the lender’s paperwork, the regular paperwork, I understand what she’s trying to do. She’ll point out the plain numbers, the dates, the signatures, the normal language, and she’ll say, “See, this is just business, this is just forms.” And I want to believe that would settle it. I really do, because it would be easier on everybody if I could just look at it and say, fine, nothing’s happening.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, and then the drone reports, and those odd numbers keep showing up in ways that don’t feel ordinary. It feels like a code. Like warnings tucked into the farm data where someone like me would see it if I was paying attention. And to me, the warning is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, yes, I think there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I haven’t figured out the whole thing, but I feel like I’ve figured out enough to know the maps and reports are telling me something.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the odd numbers are coded warnings ever completely gone away, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t gone away. I know I probably sound like I’m repeating myself, but that’s the truth of it. Even when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender’s paperwork — the plain paperwork, the kind with the dates and signatures and regular numbers — I can look at it and understand what she’s pointing to. I can see that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers don’t feel random to me. They show up like warnings, like coded warnings. And to me, they’re about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nI might stop talking about it for a while, or I might try to leave it alone so we can get through supper or get some sleep. But completely gone away? No. I don’t think it has.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you didn't believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been even a brief moment where I didn’t believe it at all. I know we keep circling that question, but I’m trying to answer it straight.\n\nThere are times I get tired of arguing about it, or I sit there while my wife lays out the lender’s paperwork and points to the plain parts — the dates, the signatures, the regular numbers — and I can see what she means on the surface. I can understand why she says, “This is just paperwork.” I’m not saying I can’t read what’s in front of me.\n\nBut it doesn’t undo what I know from the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. And the warning is about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had moments where I shut my mouth about it, or tried to act like I was letting it go so the house could be quiet for a while. But not believing it at all? No. I can’t honestly say that’s ever happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people trying to take your farm are connected to any other events or conspiracies in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it other conspiracies exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than I can prove. But it does feel connected to the farm and the people around the farm business, yes.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is this: my wife can show me the lender’s paperwork, the plain paperwork, and I can sit there and look at the numbers they put on the page, the signatures, the terms, all of that. I understand what she’s saying when she says it’s just normal lender business. I’m not blind to what’s on the paper.\n\nBut then those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports line up in a way that feels like they’re meant to be read. Like somebody put warnings there because they knew I’d see them if I looked close enough. And the warning is always pointing the same direction to me — that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, connected to everything in my life? I don’t know. I don’t want to say that. But connected to the farm, the reports, the lender stuff, the people who would benefit if I lost it? Yes. That part feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in the coded warnings ever wavered to the point where you questioned its validity for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, not for more than a few hours. I mean… I can sit there and question myself in the sense that I know how it sounds to other people. My wife will put the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’ll say, “Look, this is plain as day, this is just the loan terms, this is just the schedule, these are normal figures.” And I can follow that. I can see the regular paperwork. I can see why she thinks it should settle the matter.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, or I look over the drone reports, and those odd numbers are still there. They don’t read like accidents to me. They’re not just numbers that happened to fall that way. They feel placed. They feel like warnings, like somebody knew I’d understand what was being shown.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had a spell where I’m tired, or I try to tell myself, “Leave it alone for now, don’t stir things up,” especially if my wife is upset or we’re just trying to get through the day. But questioning whether it’s valid for more than a few hours? No. It comes right back to the same thing for me — those coded warnings are there, and they’re about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a single moment when you did not believe the odd numbers were coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No. I know that’s probably frustrating because you’re asking it very plainly, but no, I can’t think of one single moment where I didn’t believe it at all.\n\nI can have moments where I’m worn down, or where my wife is sitting at the kitchen table with the lender’s papers spread out, pointing at the regular figures and saying, “See, this is just ordinary paperwork.” And I can understand the plain part of that. I’m not saying the regular paperwork isn’t there. I see it.\n\nBut completely stop believing the odd numbers are warnings? They feel like they were put there for a reason, like somebody knew I’d catch it if I looked close enough.\n\nSo maybe I’ve gone quiet about it for a day. Maybe I’ve tried not to bring it up, just to keep peace in the house. But inside, no, I haven’t stopped believing it. To me, those coded warnings are still pointing to the same thing — folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been any amount of time, no matter how short, when you did not believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… no, I don’t think there’s been any amount of time where I didn’t believe it at all. No, Doctor. Not entirely. I keep trying to answer you plain, because I can tell you’re asking if there was even a little window where I said, “No, that’s not real,” and… I just can’t say there was.

There are times I don’t want to talk about it anymore. There are times my wife lays out the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’s patient at first, showing me the regular numbers and saying, “This is just what they sent, this is the loan, this is normal.” And I can see that part. I’m not saying the paper isn’t real or that the signatures aren’t there.

But that doesn’t change what’s in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers still stand out to me. They still feel like coded warnings. And every time I look at them, it comes back to the same thing — folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.

So I might go quiet. I might stop arguing because I’m tired or because she’s upset. But entirely stop believing it, even briefly? No. I don’t think that’s happened.

They can see the numbers, yes. But they don’t seem to understand what they mean. It feels like a pattern that’s been put there, like coded warnings. And to me, those warnings are about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might hesitate saying it out loud because I know how it sounds. I see what’s underneath it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that the odd numbers in the yield maps and drone reports are coded warnings about someone trying to take your farm?\n\nPatient: About… I’d say about 11 months now. I started noticing the odd numbers in the yield maps, and then when I saw similar things in the drone reports, it got harder and harder to ignore.\n\nBut if you’re asking how long I’ve believed they were coded warnings about someone trying to take the farm, then yes, about 11 months. That’s when it really settled in for me that it wasn’t just ordinary figures on a page.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the odd numbers were coded warnings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s been even a day where I truly didn’t think that. I’ve had days where I tried not to look at the maps, or I told myself, just get through chores, don’t start in on it again, don’t upset everybody. And my wife, she’ll sit me down with the lender’s papers and point to the plain parts — signatures, totals, dates, all that — and she’ll say, “See, this is just what they send everybody.”\n\nAnd I hear her. I do. I’m not saying I don’t hear her. But it doesn’t change what I see in the yield maps, and then in the drone reports too. Those odd numbers show up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental. They’re warnings. They’re coded warnings about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might have a quiet day, or a day where I don’t say much about it. But a day where I actually believed, “No, that’s not what this is”? No. I can’t say I’ve had that.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment when your belief in the coded warnings completely changed, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… not completely. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get worn down and I think, “Maybe I should just let it alone for tonight,” especially when my wife has the lender’s paperwork spread out in front of me and she’s showing me the regular parts of it, the plain numbers and signatures and dates and all that. She’ll say, “Look, this is just paperwork, this is just what they send,” and I can sit there and nod because I don’t want another fight.\n\nBut inside, it doesn’t really change. Not all the way. Even when she shows me that plain paperwork, I still know what I saw in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers aren’t just nothing to me. They line up too much. But if you mean did I ever actually stop believing it, even briefly… no. It’s still there. It doesn’t switch off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the coded warnings is part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do think it’s part of something bigger. She’ll point out the plain numbers, the dates, the signatures, the normal language, and she’ll say, “See, this is just business, this is just forms.” And I want to believe that would settle it. I really do, because it would be easier on everybody if I could just look at it and say, fine, nothing’s happening.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, and then the drone reports, and those odd numbers keep showing up in ways that don’t feel ordinary. It feels like a code. Like warnings tucked into the farm data where someone like me would see it if I was paying attention. And to me, the warning is that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, yes, I think there’s a larger scheme. Maybe I haven’t figured out the whole thing, but I feel like I’ve figured out enough to know the maps and reports are telling me something.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that the odd numbers are coded warnings ever completely gone away, even if only for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t gone away. I know I probably sound like I’m repeating myself, but that’s the truth of it. Even when my wife sits me down and shows me the lender’s paperwork — the plain paperwork, the kind with the dates and signatures and regular numbers — I can look at it and understand what she’s pointing to. I can see that part.\n\nBut it doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers don’t feel random to me. They show up like warnings, like coded warnings. And to me, they’re about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nI might stop talking about it for a while, or I might try to leave it alone so we can get through supper or get some sleep. But completely gone away? No. I don’t think it has.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a moment, no matter how brief, when you didn't believe in the coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there’s been even a brief moment where I didn’t believe it at all. I know we keep circling that question, but I’m trying to answer it straight.\n\nThere are times I get tired of arguing about it, or I sit there while my wife lays out the lender’s paperwork and points to the plain parts — the dates, the signatures, the regular numbers — and I can see what she means on the surface. I can understand why she says, “This is just paperwork.” I’m not saying I can’t read what’s in front of me.\n\nBut it doesn’t undo what I know from the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers… they aren’t just odd to me. They’re placed in a way that means something. They’re warnings. And the warning is about people trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had moments where I shut my mouth about it, or tried to act like I was letting it go so the house could be quiet for a while. But not believing it at all? No. I can’t honestly say that’s ever happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people trying to take your farm are connected to any other events or conspiracies in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it other conspiracies exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than I can prove. But it does feel connected to the farm and the people around the farm business, yes.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is this: my wife can show me the lender’s paperwork, the plain paperwork, and I can sit there and look at the numbers they put on the page, the signatures, the terms, all of that. I understand what she’s saying when she says it’s just normal lender business. I’m not blind to what’s on the paper.\n\nBut then those odd numbers in the yield maps and the drone reports line up in a way that feels like they’re meant to be read. Like somebody put warnings there because they knew I’d see them if I looked close enough. And the warning is always pointing the same direction to me — that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo, connected to everything in my life? I don’t know. I don’t want to say that. But connected to the farm, the reports, the lender stuff, the people who would benefit if I lost it? Yes. That part feels connected.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in the coded warnings ever wavered to the point where you questioned its validity for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, not for more than a few hours. I mean… I can sit there and question myself in the sense that I know how it sounds to other people. My wife will put the lender’s paperwork right in front of me, and she’ll say, “Look, this is plain as day, this is just the loan terms, this is just the schedule, these are normal figures.” And I can follow that. I can see the regular paperwork. I can see why she thinks it should settle the matter.\n\nBut then I go back to the yield maps, or I look over the drone reports, and those odd numbers are still there. They don’t read like accidents to me. They’re not just numbers that happened to fall that way. They feel placed. They feel like warnings, like somebody knew I’d understand what was being shown.\n\nSo maybe I’ve had a spell where I’m tired, or I try to tell myself, “Leave it alone for now, don’t stir things up,” especially if my wife is upset or we’re just trying to get through the day. But questioning whether it’s valid for more than a few hours? No. It comes right back to the same thing for me — those coded warnings are there, and they’re about folks trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a single moment when you did not believe the odd numbers were coded warnings at all?\n\nPatient: No. I know that’s probably frustrating because you’re asking it very plainly, but no, I can’t think of one single moment where I didn’t believe it at all.\n\nI can have moments where I’m worn down, or where my wife is sitting at the kitchen table with the lender’s papers spread out, pointing at the regular figures and saying, “See, this is just ordinary paperwork.” And I can understand the plain part of that. I can see the dates, the terms, the signatures, all the normal-looking numbers they want a person to focus on.\n\nBut that doesn’t erase what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers are still there, and they don’t feel random to me. They feel coded. They feel like warnings meant to tell me that folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.\n\nSo I might get quiet about it. I might stop arguing for a while because I’m tired or because I don’t want another fight. But not believe it, even for one moment? No. I can’t honestly say that.\n\nDoctor: Has there been any point where you completely stopped believing in the coded warnings for at least a day?\n\nPatient: No, not for a whole day. I don’t think there’s been even close to that, really.\n\nI mean, I’ve tried to put it down for a day, if that’s what you mean. Like when my wife is tired of hearing it, or she brings out the lender’s paperwork and says, “Here, look, this is all plain business, this is what they sent, this is what you signed, this is just the loan.” And I can sit there and follow what she’s showing me. I’m not saying the regular paperwork isn’t there. I see it.\n\nBut completely stop believing the odd numbers are warnings? No, Doctor… I don’t think there’s been a moment like that. Not with no belief whatsoever.

I know we’ve gone around this a few times, and I’m trying not to sound like I’m just repeating myself, but the truth is the truth. Even when my wife sits me down with the lender’s paperwork — the plain paperwork, the normal loan papers, the signatures and amounts and all that — I can look at it and understand what she’s saying. I’m not saying those papers aren’t there.

But that doesn’t wipe out what I see in the yield maps and the drone reports. Those odd numbers are still there, and they don’t feel ordinary to me. They feel like coded warnings. Like somebody put them in there on purpose, because they knew I’d notice.

So, no… I may stop talking about it for a while, or try to let it rest because people get upset with me, but inside I haven’t had a moment where I didn’t believe it at all. To me, it still means folks are trying to take the farm out from under me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… they brought me in because of what happened at the fair.
